Find Small Business Grants Close to Home – Not in Washington

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Look for small business grants close to home, from city and state economic development offices, utilities, corporations, and foundations. The SBA gives no grants to start or expand a business, and federal grants for companies mostly fund research or exporting. Apply only where your business fits the grant’s purpose.

A grant is the cheapest money a business can get, since you never repay it and no one takes a share of the company.

The U.S. Small Business Administration “does not provide grants for starting and expanding a business,” according to its own grants page. The federal grants a company can win go mostly to research, through the Small Business Innovation Research (SBIR) and Small Business Technology Transfer (STTR) programs, and to exporters.

So for a shop, a restaurant, or a service business, the grants worth chasing are local.

Take a bakery owner who searches the federal grant listings and finds research and export programs, nothing for a bakery. Her city’s economic development office runs a storefront program that pays back half the cost of exterior work, up to $10,000. She applies before hiring anyone, as the program requires, wins a grant, spends $16,000 on a new sign, awning, and lighting, and gets $8,000 back once the work is done.

Here’s where to look, starting close to home, with what each source pays for and who can apply.

A real government grant never charges you a fee. The Federal Trade Commission says no agency will reach out to demand that you pay for a grant, and it warns against paying any up-front fee.

It also warns against paying for a list of grants, since the complete list of federal grants on Grants.gov is free.

Check the sender, too. The SBA says it writes only from email addresses ending in @sba.gov, and that anyone claiming to be the agency from another address should be reported as fraud.

Read what the grant pays for first, since many fund a single purpose. Philadelphia’s Storefront Improvement Program covers exterior work such as signs, awnings, lighting, and paint, and STEP money goes only to export activities the SBA approves.

Then check what you’d have to put in yourself. Philadelphia’s program pays back up to half the cost of approved work, so you cover at least half and pay the whole bill until the city reimburses you. USDA’s Value-Added Producer Grants require a dollar-for-dollar match.

Many grants also want a business that’s already running. STEP is for established small businesses, and the $25,000 Wells Fargo grants the Manhattan Chamber of Commerce offered in 2026 required two years in business.

The competition is steep. Boston’s storefront program says it lacks the money to give a grant to every eligible business, and Hiring Our Heroes names five winners a round. Winning takes an application that answers the funder’s scoring criteria point by point.

Start with one call to your city’s economic development office, and ask which grants it’s funding this year, what each one pays for, and how much of the cost you’d have to cover.

15 Cities Where Rent Still Covers a Rental’s Mortgage – Then Check Taxes & Insurance

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Buy your next rental where rent is high against home prices. In 15 Midwest and Southern metros, a typical house rents for 7.4% to 11.2% of its value a year. Before you offer, check the city’s taxes, insurance, and vacancy, and what houses on that street actually rent for.

That share, a year’s rent divided by the price, is the gross yield, and it decides whether rent can cover a mortgage. In Zillow’s October 2026 figures, a typical house in the San Diego area rents for about 5% of its value a year, while one in the McAllen, Texas, area rents for 11.2%.

Now figure the mortgage payment on each, with 25% down and a 30-year loan at 7%. A typical McAllen-area house, worth $189,000, rents for about $1,760 a month, roughly $820 more than its $943 payment. A typical San Diego-area house, worth about $1 million, rents for about $4,130, about $860 short of its payment of nearly $5,000.

At those terms, the typical rent is higher than the mortgage payment in all 15 metros below, by about $270 a month in Little Rock up to about $820 in McAllen. That margin still has to pay the property taxes and insurance, plus the repairs, empty months, and management every landlord budgets for, so those costs decide whether a house makes money.

Since October 2023, typical rents have risen faster than typical home prices, so the yield on a typical U.S. house has climbed from 7.0% to 7.4%.

Each price, rent, and yield here covers a whole metro area and uses Zillow’s typical single-family home value and rent, the middle of each market. Every metro on the list passed six tests:

Two big Texas metros fell short. San Antonio’s typical rent rose only 0.7% over the past year, and Census’s metro estimates put Dallas’s rental vacancy at 14.7% this summer, nearly twice the national rate.

Here they are, from the highest yield down, with each metro’s typical house value and monthly rent.

Property taxes come out of the rent every year, and several states on this list tax a rental more heavily than an owner’s home. Texas’s homestead exemptions, which shield part of a home’s value from tax, go only to an owner’s main home, so a rental in McAllen or El Paso is taxed on its full value, the state comptroller explains. Michigan charges a rental up to 18 mills, or $18 per $1,000 of taxable value, in school operating taxes that an owner’s home skips, according to its Treasury Department.

In South Carolina, a rental is assessed at 6% of its value, against 4% for an owner’s home, and pays school operating taxes the owner’s home skips, as Richland County, home to Columbia, explains. Indiana caps a rental’s yearly property tax at 2% of its assessed value, against 1% for an owner’s home, according to the state’s Department of Local Government Finance.

Insurance is the other big cost that varies by state. In Insurance.com’s 2026 study of quoted rates for a standard policy with $300,000 of coverage on the house, Nebraska, Oklahoma, and Kansas averaged more than $5,000 a year, about twice the $2,872 national average. Texas averaged $4,582. Get a quote on a landlord policy for the exact house before you offer.

Landlord law matters as well. Oregon, Washington, and California cap yearly rent increases statewide, while 36 states bar their cities from adopting rent control at all, according to the National Apartment Association, a landlords’ trade group.

Every number above describes a whole metro, which mixes blocks where houses rent quickly with blocks where they sit empty. Before you offer, compare the rent the seller quotes with what similar houses nearby actually rent for. If you’re buying in another state, have a local agent or property manager pull those rents for you.

Start with the two or three metros whose typical prices fit your budget, and run one real listing in each through the mortgage test above, using its own tax bill and insurance quote.

MU will have to pay a fortune in the United States to recruit Pochettino

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Manchester United will have to put their hands in their pockets if they wish to recruit Mauricio Pochettino. While the future of Michael Carrick on the bench of the Red Devils seems more and more compromised, the United States coach appears to be the big favorite of the Glazer family to succeed him. But according to information from The i Paperthe Mancunian club will have to pay 9 million euros to release the Argentine coach from his contract with the American selection. A significant sum for a 54-year-old technician who recently signed a new four-year contract. Manchester United leaders are due to meet this Wednesday in Monaco to make a decision regarding Michael Carrick, whose departure could be announced as early as Thursday. With only one victory in the first ten days of the Premier League, a first in 53 years for the club, the former midfielder would not have convinced his management to give him more time. Already approached by Manchester United in 2018 and 2022, Pochettino finally saw Ole Gunnar Solskjaer and Erik ten Hag being preferred to him. This time, Joel and Avram Glazer would be determined to make the Argentinian their priority.

However, it remains to convince Mauricio Pochettino to leave the United States, where he seems fully invested in his project. The former Tottenham and Chelsea coach is said to be happy with his current situation, but would agree to listen to a possible Mancunian offer. No official approach has yet been made, and several people close to the matter doubt his desire to join Manchester United in such a complicated sporting context. The Glazers could nevertheless benefit from their relationship with American billionaire Ken Griffin, who helped finance Pochettino’s contract with the American selection and maintains historic financial ties with the owners of the Red Devils. At the same time, two other candidates remain seriously considered by management. Eddie Howe, former Newcastle manager, is said to be the favorite of director of football Jason Wilcox, while Julian Nagelsmann, former Germany manager, is said to be favored by head of recruitment Christopher Vivell. The choice of the future coach could thus depend as much on financial negotiations as on Manchester United’s ability to convince its priority candidate.

He is also receiving backlash for sharing a message from behind bars, delivered by his wife, Laura Brand, on his YouTube channel on Nov. 15 where he claimed he was innocent.

Russell Brand is selling merchandise calling for his freedom while he’s behind bars after being sentenced to 10 years and six months in prison after being found guilty of rape and sexual assault.

It is unclear how long the items have been available for purchase on Reborn, however the earliest reviews on the varying products date to Nov. 16 (he was sentenced Nov. 9). Brand recently received backlash for sharing a message from prison, delivered by his wife, Laura Brand, on his YouTube channel where he claimed he was innocent. “So Russell has asked me to read something to you today,” the actor’s wife said at the start of the video, before looking to a notebook to read her husband’s message. “Beloved brothers and sisters in Christ, God bless you for your support. As you know, I am not guilty for the offenses I have been jailed for, so something strange is happening.”

Brand was sentenced to 10 years and six months in prison after being found guilty of rape and sexual assault. The trial lasted four weeks at Southwark Crown Court, with the jury finding him guilty on one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault (he was found not guilty on two other rape charges and not guilty of attempted indecent assault).

The actor has three items featuring “Free Russell” available for purchase on the website for Reborn, his wellness business. Every item is available for $34.99; a shirt with the words “Free Russell Brand“; a hat with the same text; and another shirt that reads “Free Russell” with an image of himself.

After the YouTube video was shared, Google, the parent company of YouTube, told BBC News it was “aware of the new content and our teams are investigating,” adding, “We suspended Russell Brand’s channel from the YouTube Partner Program in September 2023. BBC News also reported that Google declined a request from the Ministry of Justice to remove the video, saying none of its rules had been broken.

His message, delivered by his wife in the YouTube video, drew criticism, with Britain’s Justice Secretary Alex Norris calling the broadcast of a statement from Brand while he is behind bars “completely unacceptable.”

“Prisoners are rightly banned from making statements that cause distress to victims,” Norris wrote on X. “This is being urgently investigated, and I expect action to be taken if he is found to have broken the rules.”

HBO Max Officially Announces New 4-Part Crime Series Premiering In Just 2 Weeks

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Another true crime series is making its way to HBO as the genre skyrockets in popularity.

The upcoming show My Favorite Murder is based on a podcast of the same name. First launched in 2016, the series features Karen Kilgariff and Georgia Hardstark discussing true crime. Their conversations combine real-life criminal cases with personal experiences and comedic commentary. The podcast quickly garnered a massive following and, a decade after its debut, it has more than two million listeners per episode.

Now, HBO is bringing the most popular true crime podcast to TV with a new documentary series. My Favorite Murder will star the podcast's hosts and is set to premiere on Thursday, December 3, 2026, at 9:00 PM ET and 600 PM PT. The four-part series will air on the network and its streaming platform HBO Max, with new episodes arriving every Thursday until December 24, 2026.

The four episodes follow Kilgariff and Hardstark to different cities, where they discuss murders, historical events, and local stories. The documentary will begin in Chicago with Kilgariff and Hardstark live at the Chicago Theatre. The debut episode investigates the murders of a hairstylist and a cosmetic surgeon. It also talks about the story of Alice Clement, a detective who worked in Chicago during the Prohibition era.

The second episode will be at the Pasadena Civic Auditorium in California. During the performance, the duo will revisit a 1980s insurance incident involving a business called Just Sweats. They also talk about the 1963 kidnapping of Frank Sinatra Jr., the son of the iconic musician.

The documentary moves to Boston for its third installment. Filmed at the Boch Center Wang Theatre, the episode examines the mysterious 1961 disappearance of Joan Risch, whose fate remains unknown to this very day. Kilgariff and Hardstark also discuss the Blizzard of 1978, a winter storm that left residents to face dangerous circumstances.

The final episode will take place at Brooklyn's Kings Theatre. The last chapter features the murder of Scarsdale diet doctor Herman Tarnower before discussing a strange event involving red bees that led investigators to a maraschino cherry warehouse.

Kilgariff and Hardstark serve as creators, hosts, writers, and executive producers of HBO's adaptation of My Favorite Murder. Alison Agosti and Page Hurwitz are also listed as writers for the show, with Hurwitz serving as showrunner and executive producer, too. Sandra Restrepo directed the first, second, and fourth episodes, while Lenn Goodside directed the third. Additional executive producers include Vikki Ernst-Chang, John Foy, Liz Garbus, Tommy Coriale, Katie King, Dan Cogan, and Jon Bardin.

My Favorite Murder will premiere on December 3, 2026, on HBO and HBO Max.

HBO Max is a subscription-based streaming service offering content from HBO, Warner Bros., DC, and more. In 2025, the service re-branded itself as HBO Max after having previously cut "HBO" from its name.

SBA Loans Cost Less Than Online Business Loans – If You Can Wait Weeks

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Choose an SBA loan over an online loan if you can wait weeks. The SBA’s guarantee comes with a capped rate and terms up to 10 years for most uses, 25 for real estate. Expect to pledge collateral on loans over $50,000, sign a personal guarantee, and, as a startup, put in at least 10% of the cost.

Owners turn to online lenders for speed. In the Federal Reserve’s 2025 Small Business Credit Survey, 60% of firms that borrowed from online lenders said their borrowing costs ran higher than expected, against 32% of large-bank borrowers.

An SBA loan is slower and cheaper. The U.S. Small Business Administration guarantees up to 85% of most 7(a) loans, which banks and other approved lenders make. In return for that backing, SBA rules cap a variable rate at the banks’ benchmark prime rate, now 7.00%, plus 3 to 6.5 percentage points, depending on the loan’s size.

Say a landscaper owes $120,000 on online term loans repaid over two years at 30% interest, about $6,710 a month. A 10-year 7(a) loan at a fixed 11% cuts that to about $1,650. The longer term costs more interest in total, about $78,000 over 10 years against $41,000 on the online loans. Extra payments shrink that, and a 7(a) loan shorter than 15 years carries no prepayment fee.

A 7(a) loan of up to $5 million can cover working capital, equipment, real estate, debt refinancing, or starting or buying a business. A 504 loan, made through a nonprofit Certified Development Company, finances buildings, land, and long-lived machinery at a fixed rate. SBA microloans of up to $50,000 come from nonprofit lenders.

Under the SBA’s lending rules, every owner of 20% or more personally guarantees the whole loan, and a loan over $50,000 needs collateral, though a lender can’t refuse you for weak collateral alone. A business open a year or less must put in at least 10% of the total cost, and so must most buyers of a business.

To get an SBA loan, start with a bank that knows your business, and ask whether it’s an SBA Preferred Lender, one allowed to approve loans without the SBA’s review, which can take up to 10 business days. Or describe your needs in the SBA’s Lender Match tool, which lists interested lenders two business days later.

Apply once you know a big cost is coming, and keep online loans for needs that can’t wait weeks.

Compare the best small business loans and SBA lenders to choose where to apply.

RLX: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Compliance and tax rules are shrinking the field, and RLX's capital discipline decides whether its European push will pay off for shareholders.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 21, 2026 at 6:57PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Rlx Technology ›

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 20, 2026, RLX (RLX -4.11%) reported net revenue of RMB 1.92 billion, a 70% year-over-year increase driven by international expansion. The company is shifting from a domestic manufacturer into a global distributor with direct access to thousands of European stores. That shift expands its reach, but it also alters its gross margin profile and changes how it allocates capital. Three things management said stand out.

Acquiring a Western European distribution platform added direct access to tens of thousands of storefronts and a business-to-business (B2B) network of independent merchants. That direct connection gives RLX immediate feedback on store inventory turnover, helping it tailor products faster than remote competitors can.

Integrating lower-margin distribution platforms compresses headline gross margins, but management generates cash to fund both acquisition opportunities and capital returns. The company evaluates share buybacks and dividends alongside acquisitions to ensure earnings-per-share (EPS) growth.

New tax rules and stricter product standards in Europe and the U.K. force noncompliant operators out of the market. RLX uses its pharmaceutical-grade research standards to navigate regulatory changes while expanding its market share across compliant retail channels.

Rapid iteration through the Shenzhen Nexus hub, the company's integrated research and manufacturing facility in China, pairs with European field teams to accelerate product rollouts.

Cash reserves of RMB 13.48 billion support distribution growth while compliance capabilities drive market share gains.

Personal Investments • Re: Wealth management

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Thanks for all the responses, really appreciate it.I’m 62 years now, I have done savings and investments in total stocks market index fund and international index funds for 25 years . I am not sure how to do tax loss harvesting, which funds to sell first before I call it quits at age 67, should I withdraw tax sheltered first or taxable. Make a trust and how to transfer funds to the kids, without estate taxes.How to combine the withdrawals to complement the social security.
Your questions are a bit complex and seem more related to taxation that what to invest in. Many "wealth managers" or "Financial Advisors" don't give great advice about taxes and typically give you general advice and say they aren't tax professionals. The correct tax answers depend a lot on your specific investments and income sources. To give good advice they should look at your previous tax returns and ask a lot of questions. There are planners that do focus on taxes and would be savvy enough to move you to a different index fund if the ones you have chosen are not tax efficient or are in a non optimal place (e.g. stocks and maybe municipal bonds should be in taxable, bonds and cash in IRA, most aggressive investments in Roth). There are a lot of circular issues with taxes when you hit certain thresholds so it can be difficult to plan. Managing your income to get ACA healthcare subsidies is another factor to think about if you retire before Medicare age and don't have a retiree medical plan.

This forum also gives good tax and income management advice and you can certainly learn here how to optimally place your assets, how to manage your income sources to minimize tax and not miss out on the 0% brackets, and how to tax loss harvest. You probably don't need a trust unless you will end up with over $15M of assets or you have an estate tax in your state with a much lower threshold. Trusts can be a hindrance and need to be updated periodically as laws and circumstances change. That costs you money each time...
Thanks for the wonderful advice. Cleared lots of questions on my mind

A Guide to NYC’s Little Italy—and the Culinary Gems Hidden Among the Tourist Traps

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Contrary to other tourist-focused neighborhoods in New York (looking at you, Times Square), Little Italy boasts an innate charm that resonates with visitors and locals alike. Sandwiched between SoHo and the Lower East Side, this jewel box area is home to long-standing, family-owned restaurants and authentic Italian bakeries, the majority of which have been neighborhood staples for decades. Yet despite its touristy atmosphere, a unique feeling of old school New York magic hangs in the air; beyond the personality-less stands and souvenir shops, framed family photos on restaurant walls and structural imperfections of tenement buildings speak to a bygone area of the city’s past, evoking a sense of nostalgia that so many neighborhoods lack. Pop down to Mulberry Street and see for yourself—though be sure you know which spots are actually legit (that’s where we come in).

A note on how we’re defining the area: Over the years, Little Italy’s boundaries have shrunk to focus on the touristic area of Mulberry Street and its immediate surroundings. While the southern and western boundaries of Canal Street and Lafayette Streets remain unchanged, the historic easterly Bowery boundary has arguably shifted to Elizabeth Street; similarly, the northerly boundary of Kenmare has also condensed down, according to many, to Broome Street. For the sake of this piece, we’re using the boundaries of Lafayette Street, Canal Street, Elizabeth Street, and Broome Street as a happy medium.

Located in the heart of Little Italy, Umberto’s Clam House is best known for its—you guessed it, all things clams. The restaurant quickly garnered attention shortly after opening in 1972, when mobster Joe "Crazy Joe" Gallo was shot in the restaurant’s dining room. Like its mafia-related history, the food at Umberto’s is as Italian-American as it gets. Baked clams and fried calamari are the appetizer list’s biggest show-stoppers, while superbly chilled clams on the half-shell pair exquisitely with equally cold (and generously sized) cocktails from the bar. Seafood-focused mains are the name of the game here, including the garlicky linguini and clam sauce and recently resurfaced scungilli with pasta served with piping hot red sauce.

Address: 132 Mulberry Street, New York, NY 10013

For Italian-American eats in a less stereotypically Little Italy ambiance, Il Cortile is the way to go. Contrary to the old school Italian vibes found in the majority of the neighborhood’s restaurants, Il Cortile’s atrium-style glass ceiling and plant-filled room feel more like dining al fresco in Trastevere than the heart of Mulberry Street. The family-owned restaurant’s neighborhood roots date back to 1975, with a solid focus on Northern Italian cuisine. The homemade cheese ravioli and wood-fired pizzas are always a safe bet, though for meat lovers, springing for the Fettuccine di Parma made with sweet sausage and wild mushrooms won’t disappoint—and be sure to save room for the pillowy soft tiramisu made with creamy mascarpone and imported Italian ladyfingers drenched in espresso.

Address: 125 Mulberry St, New York, NY 10013

Original Vincent’s, often called The Original Vincent’s Clam Bar, is home to one of the richest restaurant success stories in Little Italy. Founded in 1904 by Giuseppe and Carmela Siano, the business began as a sidewalk street cart serving up fresh seafood and jars of homemade red sauce, available in mild, medium, and hot. Today, the restaurant sits on the corner of Mott and Hester Streets, and is still known for its signature gravies, baked clams, and linguini-based pasta dishes (don’t skip the frutti di mare). As in its earliest days, Original Vincent’s still sells jars of their red sauce to-go, making replicating the restaurant’s pasta dishes at home a breeze—simply grab a bottle of Chianti, cue up your Louis Primo playlist and voila.

Paesano of Mulberry Street is home to one of the coziest interiors in Little Italy, offering a peaceful refuge from the neighborhood bustle. Rustic twig bunches and leafy plants strung with twinkle lights hang from the ceiling, while traditional Italian frescos and framed family photographs adorn the whitewashed brick walls. Originally opened in 1961, the restaurant remains owned by the same family and continues to be known for their Southern Italian recipes, including linguini with clam sauce, wood-fired pizzas, and savory chicken marsala drenched in sautéed mushrooms. Meat lovers, don’t skip the restaurant’s signature Veal A La Paesano, featuring a juicy and tender veal base layered with thin slices of prosciutto, steamed broccoli, and gooey mozzarella cheese finished with succulent brown gravy.

Address: 136 Mulberry St, New York, NY 10013

For serious pizza lovers, a trip to Lombardi’s is a no-brainer. Tucked on the corner of Mott and Spring, this neighborhood institution is credited as America’s first pizzeria, originally founded by Gennaro Lombardi in 1905. Lombardi quickly rose to fame for his large, heavily-topped pies, which he baked in an on-site, coal-and-gas oven. The result? Smoky crusts topped with perfectly balanced tomato sauce and gobs of whole milk mozzarella—along with sweet Italian sausage and various other accoutrements. The interior oozes with classic New York charm, featuring tables clad in red-and-white checkered cloths and framed family photographs strung along the red-hued walls. Head’s up, the spot is cash only, and while there’s an ATM on site, we recommend coming prepared to avoid the withdrawal fee.

Address: 30 Spring St, New York, NY 10012

Pasquale Jones-12_2. Brussels Sprout Pizza with Red Onion, Smoked Mozzarella & Lemon

In a sea of mediocre neighborhood wine lists, Pasqule Jones offers a breath of fresh air for Little Italy exploring wine lovers. Situated on the cusp of the neighborhood’s boundaries (and easily identifiable by its rainbow neon lights), this Italian-American pizzeria first opened its doors in 2016 and quickly became a staple for industry folks and visiting oenophiles alike. Beloved for its wood-fired New York style pizzas (don’t miss the signature clam pie) and thick, homemade pastas, the restaurant’s warm-yet-casual ambiance is ideal for posting up with a pie and a bottle—especially if you’re after great Burgundy, Champagne, or Italian reds. Expect classic picks from Pierre-Yves Colin-Morey, Domaine de l’Arlot, and Vietti, as well ample fun and fresh selections from Sandlands, Chacra, and Domaine Gramenon.

Address: 187 Mulberry St, New York, NY 10012

Address: 195 Grand St, New York, NY 10013

Although less famous than nearby Ferrara, Caffé Palermo features the innate Italian charm one looks for when wandering the neighborhood. Owned and operated by Baby John DeLutro (the self-proclaimed “Cannoli King”) since 1973, the establishment lives up to its owner’s moniker, offering flavored iterations—including pistachio and hazelnut made with sweet ricotta cream, the former of which is one of the joint’s standouts—in perfectly crisp shells. What Palermo does have over Ferrara is a solid amount of outdoor seating, ideal for posting up with a cappuccino and people watching on Mulberry.

Address: 148 Mulberry St, New York, NY 10013

If you’re looking for a wine break between pizza and pastries, this is just the ticket. Located on Little Italy's western limits (Centre Street between Broome and Grand), this French-inspired wine bar offers extensive by-the-glass options and shareable bites, including cheese and charcuterie boards to larger main courses, including beef tartare and lemon spaghetti. The comprehensive wine list, which features anywhere from 750 to 1,000+ references at all times, leans unsurprisingly French, though ample selections from Italy, Spain, and the USA are also available. Our suggestion? Post up with a bottle of Grower Champagne and relish in the perfectly executed Parisian bar à vin ambiance.

Address: 249 Centre St, New York, NY 10013

Address: 409 Broome St, New York, NY 10013

Technically located on the cusp of Little Italy’s historic boundaries (Kenmare and Broome Streets), La Esquina offers a flavor-packed reprieve from the classic red-sauce joints synonymous with the neighborhood. Situated in a former counter deli, this multi-level Mexican hotspot is best known for its casual taqueria (located in ‘The Corner Deli’ space) serving tacos, tortas, and ceviche. However, for those looking for something a bit stronger, head through the secret door and down the stairs to find the restaurant’s underground speakeasy. Lit by candle and adorned with Lucha Libre posters, the bar’s high-energy ambiance is prime for enjoying queso fundido and smoky Mexican street corn (elotes callejeros) washed down with expertly crafted agave-based cocktails.

Address: 114 Kenmare St, New York, NY 10012

As the most discerning, up-to-the-minute voice in all things travel, Condé Nast Traveler is the global citizen’s bible and muse, offering both inspiration and vital intel. We understand that time is the greatest luxury, which is why Condé Nast Traveler mines its network of experts and influencers so that you never waste a meal, a drink, or a hotel stay wherever you are in the world.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/regulatory/cms-issues-medicare-advantage-proposals-aimed-boosting-competition" hreflang="en">CMS issues Medicare Advantage proposals aimed at boosting competition</a>

📅 2026-11-20  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →
CMS issues Medicare Advantage proposals aimed at boosting competition" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

The Trump administration has proposed a slew of changes to Medicare Advantage and Part D designed to "revitalize care" in these markets.

For one, the Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services said late Friday that it will update application and network adequacy requirements to make it easier for new entrants. The agency proposed to require organizations to demonstrate network adequacy during their first year in operation, rather than during the application process.

This shift would eliminate a significant barrier for new entrants, driving greater competition in the market.

CMS has also proposed combining three specialty areas in behavioral health — clinical psychology, clinical social work and outpatient behavioral health — into one specialty group for the purposes of network adequacy. The changes will give plans greater flexibility in building out behavioral health networks.

CMS will also make adjustments that seek to boost competition for special needs plans.

In addition, the agency said it will make adjustments to the star ratings program, focusing its ratings on 15 measures. The proposed measure set would emphasize "clinical outcomes and patient experience while retaining select process measures in high-priority areas where performance remains low."

CMS is also aiming to update the calculation methodology that will "raise the bar for achieving high ratings," and thus quality bonus payments.

"People with Medicare should be able to choose coverage based on what matters most to them — quality, affordability, and access," said CMS Administrator Mehmet Oz, M.D., in the announcement. "These proposals are about creating a Medicare Advantage marketplace that rewards better care and better value while empowering beneficiaries with the options and knowledge they need to make informed decisions about their health."

The proposed rule also includes provisions that align with Department of Health and Human Services Secretary Robert F. Kennedy's Make America Healthy Again agenda by setting a "healthy food" standard that MA plans should follow for supplemental benefits.

The goal, CMS said, is to ensure that Medicare dollars are spent on food benefits that align with MAHA.

"Medicare food benefits should pay for nutritious food that helps people improve their health and manage chronic disease," Kennedy said in the announcement. "This proposal would require those benefits to meet the Dietary Guidelines for Americans while preserving access to foods and nutritional products that providers recommend for patients’ specific medical needs."

"Under President Trump, we are putting prevention at the center of health care and demanding more from every Medicare dollar," Kennedy said.

15 Best American Movies Of All Time Ranked, Including Once Upon A Time...In Hollywood

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

There have been approximately 500,000 feature-length films released to date, spanning several genres including action, drama, comedy, sci-fi, and more. This total includes titles released in Hollywood, along with other major cinema markets like India (Bollywood) and China (with its ever thriving box office). Of this total, around 50,000 of these are major American movies (which includes studio films, independent projects, and more).

The United States is often considered to be the hub for cinema, with the longstanding presence of this business in cities like New York, Chicago, and Los Angeles (especially the latter). Some of the biggest movies ever made have been in the U.S., with franchises like Star Wars and the Marvel Cinematic Universe both being funded and made by Americans.

Not only does the United States have some of the biggest movies, but the country is also home to some of the very best movies made as well. Titles like Citizen Kane, The Godfather, and the original Star Wars trilogy are all products of American auteurs from U.S.-based studios. Not only that, but these successful projects span nearly a century, with films like the aforementioned Citizen Kane debuting roughly 85 years ago (and with plenty of amazing movies still being made in modern times, too).

When looking at the best American movies ever made, other titles come to mind as well. Films from directors like Steven Spielberg, Quentin Tarantino, and the Coen brothers, along with classics like The Wizard of Oz and It’s a Wonderful Life, all deserve to be in contention for this prestigious list.

Once again, one of the very best films ever made is Citizen Kane, which was released in 1941. Although it may have made its debut before the United States even officially entered World War II, and despite its lack of color, this Orson Welles masterpiece is the kind of movie that is way ahead of its time in virtually every aspect.

Citizen Kane, which was written, directed by, and starring Orson Welles, tells the story of the life of Charles Foster Kane. Kane’s life, from childhood to old age, is portrayed in the film, and includes his rise to owner of the New York Inquirer and an ambitious run for Governor. All of this is told within the framework of a mystery presented in the film’s very first scene, which shows Kane’s death, and his very famous final words: “Rosebud.” Citizen Kane then explores that mystery and teases the cryptic meaning behind them.

There are many reasons why Citizen Kane is such a classic. Welles’ acting is exceptional, as is his innovative and industry-changing direction. There’s also the usage of non-linear storytelling, which had seldom, if ever, been done in mainstream movies up until this point. Citizen Kane popularized this tool, thus paving the way for countless classic non-linear movies (Christopher Nolan’s movies come to mind here, as the director is known for his affection for this kind of narrative).

Stanley Kubrick was an English director, but one of his most famous films, 2001: A Space Odyssey, was made by an American studio and stars American actors. As such, 2001 more than belongs on this list, and deserves its place among the best American movies ever made. This movie is truly extraordinary, with visuals that are still impressive decades later. Additionally, Kubrick’s hard-to-wrap your head around story deserves great praise, too, as it paved the way for other filmmakers, like David Lynch, to explore the abstract in their own projects.

Some movies on this list were overlooked upon their initial release, only to receive acclaim in the years that followed. Others, meanwhile, were praised right away, and 1991s The Silence of the Lambs belongs firmly in that category. The film, which stars Jodie Foster and Anthony Hopkins, won the “Big Five” awards at the Oscars — Best Picture, Best Actor, Best Actress, and Best Adapted Screenplay.

And, just like many other films on this list, the story of The Silence of the Lambs is likely very familiar. The film follows FBI trainee Clarice Starling as she’s tasked with meeting the infamous serial killer Hannibal Lecter, with the hope that Hopkins’ character will offer some insight that may lead to the capture of another killer, Buffalo Bill, who’s still on the loose.

This movie remains one of the best American films ever made, even 35 years after its release, in large part because of the performances by Foster and Hopkins. Both give some of the best turns in recent Hollywood history, and performances that have greatly influenced the future of the industry, too. For example, Hopkins’ portrayal of a serial killer has become the basis for actors playing murderers ever since.

Halloween is one of the simplest films on this list, with the story of a masked killer returning to his hometown and wreaking havoc among the residents, who fight to stay alive amidst his murder spree. Yet, that simplicity doesn’t go against John Carpenter’s film, which is not just one of the best horror movies ever, but one of the best films ever, too.

The straightforwardness of Halloween’s narrative is actually one of the film’s greatest strengths, as it allows the characters, like Michael Myers, Dr. Loomis, and Laurie Strode, the chance to truly shine. In turn, the suspense and terror of the film’s story is allowed to grow, too. Ultimately, Halloween (1978) is a masterpiece in horror storytelling, with expertly executed scares and a main villain that remains one of the best movie characters ever.

Steven Spielberg has made so many classic films in his career, but one that stands near (or at the very top) of the list is E.T. The Extra-Terrestrial. The 1982 film focuses on a young boy who discovers a gentle alien stranded on Earth, and the two then bond as they evade government officials and attempt to send E.T. home.

This movie truly has it all — it’s funny, relatable (with an excellent portrayal of kids at this age), and, above all else, extremely emotional. The film’s ending, where E.T. goes home on his spaceship, is simultaneously heartwarming and heartbreaking at the same time, even all these years later.

Actor James “Jimmy” Stewart served in the United States military during World War II and, just one year after the end of the conflict, starred in what proved to be the biggest film of his career (and one often watched during this time of year) — It’s a Wonderful Life. The film, which is loosely based on Charles Dickens’ A Christmas Carol, is essentially the perfect holiday movie.

The story follows George Bailey, a down-on-his-luck man who plans to take his own life on Christmas Eve. However, his plans are thwarted when he’s visited by a Guardian Angel, who shows him why life is still worth living. It’s a Wonderful Life is one of the most emotionally touching movies ever made, making it one of the best movies ever made, period, in turn.

Casablanca may be named after a city in Morocco, but it’s firmly an American film, and one of the country’s very best, in fact. The film, which was released in 1943 (at the height of World War II) and stars Humphrey Bogart, has the classic redemption arc for its leading character. Bogart’s Rick Blaine claims that he won’t risk his life for any cause (or anyone), but, over the course of the movie, changes his mind and becomes a staunch anti-fascist.

It’s the kind of character arc that has been repeated countless times since, perhaps most notably in the very first Star Wars film, which saw Han Solo go from being ambivalent towards the Empire to becoming a full-blown Rebel hero. This makes Casablanca not only an incredible movie, but very influential as well. Not only that, but the film is also beautifully made, with exceptional acting, writing (excellent dialogue in particular), and cinematography.

Bar The Godfather trilogy, Francis Ford Coppola’s most famous work is arguably 1979s Apocalypse Now. The film had a tumultuous production, but these hardships certainly paid off in the end. Apocalypse Now is, in so many ways, the perfect war movie. It shows the harrowing realities of the conflict in Vietnam, and gets deep into the psyche of the characters at the heart of the story.

The Coen Brothers’ best film, Fargo remains a masterclass in tone, suspense, and humor, even thirty years later. The film, which is set in the frozen town of Fargo, North Dakota, focuses on a world of crime that comes to the small town after a car salesman hires hitmen to kill his wife. The plot may sound dark, but the Coen Brothers inject the film with so much life that it actually ends up being very funny, emotional, and, above all else, a true whirlwind of emotions.

The Wizard of Oz had plenty of reported issues during production, including drug use, infighting among the cast and crew, and going over budget. Yet, none of that affected the final product, which remains one of the best American movies ever made. The story of Dorothy and her dog Toto traveling to Oz, meeting a group of friends, and attempting to get back to Kansas is simply timeless. It feels like a modern-day fairy tale in this regard, and this movie is so good that it’s no wonder that the franchise is still going nearly 90 years later.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Why International First Class Has Failed At US Airlines (But Shouldn’t)

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We’ve just seen American formally retire its Boeing 777 first class, and with that, no airline in the United States has a “true” international first class anymore. It’s the end of an era, because going back many decades, premium products were largely pioneered by US carriers. So I think this raises the question — why can US carriers not make first class work in a way that’s economically viable, especially as they keep talking about how premium they are?

Before we get into this, let me make sure we’re all on the same page when it comes to terms. This post isn’t about the premium cabin on domestic flights, which is often referred to as first class. Instead, it’s about international flights that have a cabin above business class, when it’s marketed as first class. So this would include airlines on my list of the world’s best first class products.

Airline executives are divided on the merits of having an international first class cabin. Some insist that there’s no need for such a cabin, as business class has improved so much. Meanwhile others absolutely see merit to such a product.

I mean, just look at how carriers like Air France, Lufthansa, Singapore Airlines, and SWISS, are all investing in first class. I think Air France’s La Premiere first class is the perfect example of the upside such a product has for an airline:

What’s interesting is that for the airlines that succeed with first class, the single biggest market — and single biggest point of sale — is the United States. So how is it that carriers like Air France, Lufthansa, SWISS, etc., can make money offering first class to the United States (primarily targeting Americans), but US airlines seemingly can’t?

The US has way too many people with tens or hundreds of millions of dollars, or even billions of dollars. There are plenty of people who would pay to fly private, and would pay for Air France La Premiere first class, but would never consider paying the high price for first class on a US carrier, because it’s just not worth it.

When they fly first class, they want this…

It’s no surprise that first class at US carriers has historically failed. In many ways, it reflects the failures of the industry from decades past. In other words, first class has long been thought of as “upgrade class,” rather than something actually worth paying for. US carriers have managed to change that narrative on domestic flights, but not so much on international flights.

What explains the failure of first class at US carriers? If you ask me, here are the main factors:

Call it a hot take, but I think international first class absolutely could succeed at a US carrier, if done right, with a couple of caveats. Let me start with the two main hurdles that would need to be overcome, and I’ll acknowledge, this might just be the biggest issue:

If those hurdles could be overcome, here’s the play, as I see it (and yes, this is largely inspired by Air France La Premiere):

I think the halo effect of something like this would be massive as well. Seriously, US airline executives love to talk about how premium they are, but they can’t even introduce a top-end product, despite having access to the most lucrative customer base in the world.

In terms of economics, let me also point out that the opportunity cost of a first class cabin isn’t actually that high. “Premium” is all the rage, so what you do here is eliminate some economy seats:

At this point, no airline from the United States offers first class on international flights anymore. For airlines that can’t stop talking about how unbelievably premium they are, I find that to be a pretty perplexing reality. I know that many people have just accepted “oh, a good business class is the future,” but I have a different take.

I think no US carrier actually being able to offer a globally competitive product is a failure in strategy and creativity. They have access to the world’s most lucrative customer base, and other airlines can seemingly make money serving US customers with a first class product. The issue is, US carriers have long not actually given people a reason to fly first class, all while offering too much capacity.

Personally I think there would be a lot of merit to an international first class product that’s boutique, and is operated in the right markets. That’s not to say that I think it’ll happen — I don’t — but I do think it could succeed. The only real hurdle to overcome would be how to ensure good service…

What’s your take on the possible merit of US carriers offering first class?

Hoymiles showcases versatile residential solar and storage solutions at RE+ 2026

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

LAS VEGAS, Nov. 20, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Hoymiles showcased its latest residential solar and energy storage solutions at RE+ 2026, featuring the HiFlow Pro plug-in microinverter, HiBattery 4020 series and HMS series microinverters. Designed for a range of household energy needs, the portfolio covers emerging plug-in PV applications, expandable home energy storage and rooftop solar, highlighting Hoymiles' continued commitment to the North American market.

HiFlow Pro brings flexibility to plug-in solar

HiFlow Pro microinverter, named to TIME's Best Inventions 2026 list, is the first plug-in microinverter in the U.S. market to meet the UL 3700 safety standard. The HiFlow Pro 1-in-1 model connects to a single solar module and weighs approximately 1.8 kg, making installation straightforward. With a startup voltage as low as 22V and peak MPPT efficiency of 99.8%, it is designed for efficient solar generation in a compact form. Built-in Wi-Fi and Bluetooth connectivity enable real-time system monitoring through the S-Miles Home app.

HiBattery 4020 X combines solar generation and storage

The HiBattery 4020 X combines solar generation and storage in one compact system, featuring a built-in 4.02 kWh battery, four MPPTs, up to 4 kW PV input and 1,200 W output. It supports Hoymiles and third-party smart meters, with MQTT connectivity for integration with external energy management platforms. The system features 48 BMS protection functions and multiple safety measures, encased in an IP66-rated enclosure. For greater storage capacity, users can add HiBattery 4020 S expansion battery stack to scale the system up to 16.08 kWh.

HMS microinverters offer flexible rooftop configurations

Hoymiles also presented three HMS microinverter models: the HMS-500-1T-NA, HMS-1000-2T-NA and HMS-2000-4T-NA. With one, two and four PV module connections respectively, the microinverters provide flexible options for different rooftop layouts.

During the exhibition, TÃV Rheinland presented Hoymiles with a functional safety test report for its HoyUltra 261A C&I BESS. The testing was conducted in accordance with IEC 60730-1:2022 Annex H and UL 60730-1:2024 Annex H, with the system receiving a "Pass" result. The report provides independent third-party testing evidence for the system's functional safety design.

With its U.S. operations and North American technical service team, Hoymiles continues to strengthen local partnerships and deliver responsive support, while developing products and services tailored to local needs to help households build more flexible and resilient energy systems.

Electric Vehicles Now Make Up Nearly 1/3rd of Global New Car Sales: Study

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The conversion of the automotive industry over to electric vehicles, sold as a foregone conclusion by many car brands in the early 2020s, may have largely stalled out in America, but the picture is far different globally. According to data from the Commonwealth Bank of Australia, EVs now account for 31 percent of all passenger vehicle sales.

And the global increase came despite falling sales in China. The major driver of electric vehicle sales has been Europe; European EV sales have risen 37 percent year over year so far in 2026, climbing to 43 percent of the new vehicle sales market. CommBank data also showed substantial growth rates in countries such as Brazil (EV sales up 164 percent, to a 13 percent market share) and India (EV sales up 76 percent, to a 9 percent market share). Even in Japan, where manufacturers have been notably EV-reticent, sales jumped 80 percent year-over-year to 5 percent of the market.

Meanwhile, electric vehicle sales have declined in the United States this year. According to Cox Automotive data, EV sales made up 6.4 percent of the new car market in Q3 of 2026, down from more than 10 percent in Q3 of 2025. Fuel prices have been elevated this year, but that has led Americans to gravitate toward hybrids rather than fully electric vehicles. Total electrified vehicles now account for nearly 25 percent of the American market.

Much of the decline has been a direct result of Trump administration policies. Last year, the U.S. eliminated a $7500 federal tax credit for electric cars, which—due to a commercial vehicle loophole—made them especially cheap for buyers to lease; the 10 percent share of sales in Q3 2025 came from a glut of sales as buyers rushed to take advantage of the tax credits before they expired. Congress is also considering imposing an annual federal tax on electric cars.

The Trump administration has also effectively eliminated enforcement of America’s CAFE standards, which would have effectively forced manufacturers to sell EVs to avoid hefty fines. American laws and tariffs also effectively prohibit importing affordable Chinese-made EVs, which have been a major reason behind the sales increase in Europe.

Selling Pressure Mounts as Bitcoin Whales Dump $3.4 Billion in BTC

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin (BTC) is facing renewed selling pressure as large holders continue to reduce their exposure, with on-chain data showing that whales have redistributed about $3.4 billion worth of the cryptocurrency over the past two weeks.

Notably, the largest cryptocurrency by market capitalization has fallen nearly 13% over the past week. The decline extends its November losses, bringing its price to a three-month low.

According to analyst Ali Martinez, large entities have sold or redistributed 48,618 BTC, valued at approximately $3.4 billion, over the past two weeks.

“Whales are still reducing their exposure. The selling hasn’t stopped yet,” Martinez tweeted.

The continued distribution suggests that major holders are yet to regain confidence in Bitcoin’s near-term prospects. With thousands of coins changing hands, the market faces the challenge of absorbing this supply without triggering further downward pressure.

Beyond whale activity, Bitcoin’s weekly performance is adding to concerns about the market’s strength.

Analyst Ted Pillows noted that the cryptocurrency has closed six consecutive weeks in the red, a pattern previously seen during bear markets. Trading below both moving averages further weakens its recovery prospects.

“Along with that, Bitcoin failed to hold both the 200W EMA and 200W MA, which is a major sign of weakness. As long as BTC stays below these levels, sellers are in full control,” he wrote.

Meanwhile, several important price levels have given way, leaving Bitcoin vulnerable to another decline.

Mookie, an advocate at Bitget Wallet and ambassador at Dinari Global, highlighted the cryptocurrency’s failure to maintain successive support levels, warning that $57,700 could come into focus if buyers fail to regain control.

“If BTC can’t get back above $66.5K. we could see another drop toward the July low at $57.7K. But if buyers push it back above $66.5K we might finally see a bounce. Still not convinced we’re done dropping,” he noted.

His comments put $66,500 at the center of Bitcoin’s immediate price outlook. Reclaiming that level could help buyers regain some momentum, while another rejection would leave the July low of $57,700 as a potential downside target.

Despite the prevailing bearish sentiment, some market observers believe Bitcoin could be approaching a turning point.

Gordon, founder of Crypto Crib and co-founder of Alphametrics, pointed to a potential inverse head-and-shoulders formation, suggesting that extreme pessimism could set the stage for a reversal.

“All longs have been wiped out, sentiment is max bearish, and retail is going heavy short. This is where reversal happens,” he commented.

An inverse head-and-shoulders pattern is a technical formation often associated with a potential bullish reversal after an extended decline. It typically consists of three troughs, with the middle one forming the lowest point.

Gordon’s assessment offers a contrasting view to the warnings from other analysts. He believes that widespread bearish positioning and the liquidation of leveraged long positions could create conditions for buyers to return.

However, the formation does not guarantee an immediate recovery. Bitcoin still needs enough buying interest to reverse its recent losses and reclaim key price levels.

At press time, BTC was trading at $59,299, down 5.40% over the past 24 hours.

Twins lose pitcher before key MLB deadline & he immediately hits free agent market

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The MLB offseason has delivered another notable development, as the Minnesota Twins are reportedly moving on from starting pitcher Bailey Ober ahead of Friday's non-tender deadline.

According to Mark Feinsand of MLB.com, a source confirmed that Minnesota will non-tender Ober, allowing the right-hander to immediately enter free agency. The organization has yet to officially announce the decision.

Ober was entering his final year of salary arbitration and was projected to earn approximately $7 million for the 2027 season. However, the Twins have apparently decided that moving on from the veteran starter is the better option.

The decision comes as teams across baseball face an important deadline to determine which arbitration-eligible and pre-arbitration players on their 40-man rosters will receive contracts for next season.

MORE: New team emerges in Tarik Skubal MLB free agent sweepstakes

Sep 26, 2026; Minneapolis, Minnesota, USA; Minnesota Twins pitcher Bailey Ober (17) delivers a pitch during the first inning against the Texas Rangers at Target Field. Mandatory Credit: Matt Krohn-Imagn Images

Ober spent all six seasons of his MLB career with Minnesota, becoming a familiar face in the team's starting rotation.

The 2026 campaign, however, was a challenging one for the right-hander. He appeared in 25 games, 24 of which were starts, finishing with a 7-7 record, a 4.47 ERA and a 1.30 WHIP.

Across 140 career appearances with the Twins, Ober recorded a 4.15 ERA.

While his experience and familiarity with the organization made him a potential candidate to remain in Minnesota, his projected arbitration salary and recent performance may have contributed to the club's decision.

The move also follows Minnesota's decision to sign right-handed pitcher Sonny Gray to a two-year contract on Wednesday, potentially creating additional competition for spots in the starting rotation.

MORE: Brewers lose World Series champion pitcher to White Sox in free agency

May 24, 2026; Boston, Massachusetts, USA; Minnesota Twins starting pitcher Bailey Ober (17) walks to the dugout after being relieved during the sixth inning against the Boston Red Sox at Fenway Park. Mandatory Credit: Bob DeChiara-Imagn Images

Friday, November 20, marks the MLB non-tender deadline, with teams required to make their decisions by 5 p.m. ET.

Players who do not receive contracts by the deadline are considered non-tendered and immediately become free agents, allowing them to negotiate with any interested organization.

For Ober, the decision represents an unexpected end to his tenure in Minnesota and an opportunity to find a fresh start elsewhere.

At 31 years old, the right-hander could still attract interest from teams looking to add starting pitching depth.

With free agency now on the horizon, Ober will have an opportunity to prove that his best baseball is still ahead of him.

10 stocks we like better than Costco Wholesale ›

However, Costco stock soared last week after the company announced that it will pay a $30 per-share special dividend to shareholders as of Nov. 24. Should you buy Costco stock now?

Costco has paid a special dividend to investors four times since 2015, and it keeps getting larger. The last one was in 2023 for $15. Costco's regular dividend yields a paltry 0.53%, and this is a fantastic addition. The latest special dividend will be paid on Dec. 4 to shareholders as of Nov. 24.

1 High-Flying Stock to Buy Hand Over Fist if the Stock Market Crashes

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Moderna's cancer vaccine data were historic, but the stock prices in a flawless future.

By Prosper Junior Bakiny – Nov 20, 2026 at 12:21PM EST

Innovation often pays off. Moderna (MRNA -0.92%) has proven as much this year. The company's shares have soared in 2026 on the back of impressive clinical and regulatory progress. But is there any upside left for the vaccine maker? Not in my view. But Moderna would be a great stock to buy if the stock market crashes. Read on to find out more.

Moderna is pioneering mRNA-based personalized cancer vaccines. These products offer the potential to train patients' immune systems to recognize and destroy cancer cells based on specific mutations present in each patient's tumor. This approach could lead to much greater treatment efficacy than conventional cancer medicines. Personalized cancer vaccines may also more effectively prevent recurrence.

Moderna provided the first positive Phase 3 clinical trial data for an investigational personalized cancer vaccine, intismeran autogene, earlier this year. Moderna is developing intismeran autogene with Merck (MRK +1.59%), and in this Phase 3 study, the personalized cancer vaccine was tested as a combination therapy with Keytruda -- the best-selling cancer drug in the world -- versus Keytruda alone in certain patients with melanoma. Intismeran autogene, combined with Keytruda, led to meaningful improvements in recurrence-free survival. This clinical win sent Moderna's shares up by more than 100% overnight.

The market is seeing beyond Moderna's clinical win. The company proved that personalized cancer vaccines can reach significant clinical success. Intismeran autogene hasn't done so yet, but based on its Phase 3 results, it should eventually generate significant sales. Some analysts expect the personalized cancer vaccine to generate more than $10 billion in revenue at its peak, especially as it earns approvals across additional indications beyond melanoma.

It is being invested in as a treatment across several other forms of cancer. And the rest of the company's lineup and pipeline also looks robust. For instance, Moderna has earned approval for mFLUSIVA, an influenza vaccine, earlier this year. Its coronavirus vaccine is still on the market, and it is developing several other candidates in infectious diseases.

Some of the company's targets look ambitious. Moderna is developing a potential HIV vaccine, for instance. There is a long road ahead for this candidate, but if it is successful, it could be a game changer. That said, Moderna's most important strength isn't any single pipeline candidate or currently approved product.

It is the company's innovative mRNA-based platform that should produce significant clinical milestones over the next five to 10 years. However, there is a problem. The market may have already factored Moderna's success into its share price. Consider that the company's market cap is $82 billion, as of writing. Even assuming Moderna could generate $10 billion in sales next year (which it won't), it would be trading at 8.2x estimated 2027 sales, a fairly expensive valuation for a biotech stock of this size.

True, more clinical wins could jolt the stock, but even with that in mind, I am not convinced the valuation makes sense. After all, even intismeran autogene isn't approved yet, and some other advanced pipeline candidates may not earn U.S. approval for another two years. Adding commercial ramp-up times, we are looking at revenue far into the future even for the company's most advanced programs, and that's assuming they are successful at all.

The uncertainty of these future revenues (and cash flows) makes for a somewhat risky outlook at current levels: Too much could go wrong and sink the stock. I'd like a much more reasonable valuation and a significant margin of error -- considering we are discussing a volatile biotech stock -- before investing. If Moderna's market cap falls below $50 billion in a market crash, I'd definitely consider buying because the business looks strong, the science is impeccable, and the company's future looks bright.

But valuation is a major sticking point. I am keeping Moderna on my watch list for now.

Bitcoin Fell More Than 20% in November. History Says This Happens Next.

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The world’s top cryptocurrency could face a lot more selling pressure.

Bitcoin's (BTC -4.84%) price has declined by over 20% in November. At just under $60,000, it's trading more than 50% below its all-time high of about $126,000 from last October. Let's see what caused that decline -- and what its historical performance suggests could happen next.

A year ago, many investors expected the Fed to cut interest rates in 2026. Lower rates would presumably weaken the U.S. dollar, making blue chip cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin more attractive, and spark a rotation from conservative, higher-yielding investments into riskier ones.

Long-term catalysts also supported Bitcoin. Its spot-price exchange-traded funds (ETFs), initially approved in early 2024, were attracting more retail and institutional investors. It was also attracting more attention from corporations and governments as a hedge against the debasement of fiat currencies. Investors also expected its next halving -- which halves the rewards for mining Bitcoin every four years -- to exacerbate its scarcity in 2028.

But in 2026, those tailwinds dissipated as the Iran war exacerbated inflation and prompted the Fed to raise its rates for the first time in three years in September. The Democratic sweep in the U.S. midterms also increased the likelihood of tighter restrictions on the crypto market. Those challenges curbed the market's appetite for cryptocurrencies, and Bitcoin's price plummeted.

Past performance never guarantees future gains, but Bitcoin has a solid record of bouncing from steep peak-to-trough declines. However, each drawdown shook out many investors.

As of this writing, Bitcoin has declined 53% from its all-time high. Therefore, investors shouldn't be surprised if it sinks even lower over the next year before stabilizing. An 80% drop from $126,000 would reduce its price to about $25,000.

That would be a painful pullback -- especially for investors who bought Bitcoin in late 2025 -- but its historical performance suggests it could bounce back sharply after it bottoms out. Every time Bitcoin crashed, it set new all-time highs within a few years.

I'm not saying that's guaranteed to happen this time, but plenty of catalysts -- including interest rate cuts and its next halving -- could drive the bulls back to Bitcoin. While it could certainly get worse before it gets better, I believe Bitcoin will be trading much higher in just a few years.

Move Over, Balaclavas: The Snood Is Set to Be Winter's Biggest Fashion Trend

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Alongside their cousin, the hooded scarf!

It was just a few years ago that the balaclava was everywhere. Fashion people referred to it as the winter headpiece you absolutely needed. But now, I’ve noticed a new star, and that’s the snood, alongside the hooded scarf. Most people over the years have blended these three headpieces, but I’m here to inform the masses: you’re probably wearing a snood or hooded scarf now, not a balaclava.

Balaclavas are a full-coverage piece of headgear that wraps around the entire head, neck, and face (leaving only the eyes, nose, or mouth exposed), whereas a snood is a continuous circular loop or tube of fabric designed primarily to insulate the neck and cover the top of the head.

Balaclavas tend to be closer to the face, similar in fit to a ski mask, while snoods are loose-fitting and have a sort of cowl design to them. According to the winter sports community, balaclavas are better for skiing and extreme cold, but for most of us, we’re just looking to warm up a little with our winter accessories.

If you’re ready to wear your own snood this winter season, I’ve gathered some stylish inspo below to help build out those mood boards.

This brown snood moment with a light beige trench coat is peak winter outfit. The headpiece pulls the whole cozy vibes together and gives ultimate cool girl.

As a Floridian girlie, shorts in the winter are my kind of speed. Throw on those uber warm tights I see scrolling down my TikTok ‘for you’ page and you’re in business. The hooded scarf just elevates the whole moment too and keeps your head warm, which is ultimately the goal.

Colorful snoods aren’t always top of the list, especially if you’re looking for a headpiece that can blend with all your outfits. But if you’re hoping to bring a bit more fun to your winter style, then grabbing a snood like this one above is the way to do it.

Monochromatic is always in, but getting super close to a full black look and throwing in a light-colored headpiece is my personal winter hack. It brightens up the whole outfit, but doesn’t a neutral color like the one above still keep the look uniform and stylish.

Why Autistic Employees Are Targeted By Bullies

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Updated November 20, 2026 | Reviewed by Tyler Woods

Workplace bullying damages health and drains productivity. For autistic employees, it perpetuates the cycle of abuse that starts much earlier. A 2026 review confirmed that autistic students experience disproportionately high levels of bullying, with most estimates falling between 40% and 70% (Heredia-Oliva et al., 2026), and it is a major source of stress for them.

The painful pattern then follows people into work. And many work environments prove fertile grounds for both overt (threatening, shouting) and especially covert (backstabbing, sabotaging,

In experiments, neurotypical people reported liking autistic people less than neurotypical people, with vague explanations like perceived “awkwardness” (Alkhaldi et al, 2021). Workplace stressors have the potential to magnify the dislike. Autistic employees, with their spiky profiles of abilities, are more likely to be on extreme ends of performance. High performers often experience veiled violence like sabotage or having crucial information withheld due to coworker envy; low performers face overt hostility, which makes their performance decline rather than improve (Jensen et al., 2014). Add autistic tendencies of direct communication, including possibly challenging inefficiencies and misconduct (Hartman & Hartman, 2024), along with a focus on work over socializing, and the target is set.

Autistic people's nervous systems often have less capacity to recover from social stress (Cheng et al., 2020), and autistic adults are already at elevated risk of developing mental and physical health issues due to a mismatch between their needs and the environments, sensory stress, effortful masking, and other forms of chronic stress (Botha & Frost, 2020; Dhanasekara et al., 2023). Past bullying also leaves a psychological trace: 87.4% of autistic adults showed signs of fear of being laughed at, compared with 22.6% of non-autistic adults (Leader et al., 2018).

Multiple vulnerabilities create compounding effects. Years of mistreatment result in hypervigilance and keep the body's threat response switched on. Each new injury lands on a system that never fully recovered from the last. To make things worse, the lack of resources often associated with unemployment, with only about 3 in 10 working-age autistic people in the UK employed (Buckland, 2024), leaves fewer options for recovery, seeking help, or leaving a toxic job.

Reporting bullying at work is generally a thankless task. Reports are often ignored or dismissed: in a nationally representative U.S. survey, 60% of employers reacted negatively when bullying was reported (Namie, 2021), and targets describe HR as regularly siding with bullies (Boddy & Boulter, 2025). Moreover, skillful manipulators, which many bullies are, can present themselves as victims.

Autistic people, however, face additional barriers to successfully reporting bullying and being heard.

Most organizations say they are committed to healthy workplaces, but their methods for handling bullying often make things worse. Those methods fail autistic employees and others dealing with trauma or overwhelm in at least three ways.

They are reactive. Action typically begins only after bullying is proven. But the first line of defense against bullying and any other hazard at work is prevention (Praslova et al., 2022). Preventing harm is both more ethical and more practical than repairing it.

They put the burden on the target. Targets are told to document what was said and done, when, and who was present, then discuss it calmly with a supervisor. How many people can realistically do this while doing their jobs, under stress, and dealing with trauma? The process appears to be designed to make it easy to reject the complaint because of the “tone” or the target “being emotional.”

They try to fix personalities. Bullies may act out their entitlement, fragile egos, and poor self-control, but bullying flourishes where the environment lets it go undetected and rewards it with resources or advancement (Praslova et al., 2022; Salin, 2003). Change the environment, and you are more likely to change the behavior.

Remove the rewards for bullying. Avoid internal competition for scarce resources. Distribute resources fairly and transparently, and balance individual rewards with team rewards. Realistic workloads reduce bullying used to offload work onto others, and transparency may take some of the fuel out of envy. Transparent decision-making makes rumors and backstabbing less likely to pay off (Praslova et al., 2022).

Set standards and train for them. A clear policy should state that bullying is not tolerated, and name its covert forms, not just its overt ones (Praslova et al., 2022). Train everyone in respectful communication, and train new managers specifically for complex interpersonal situations. Ideally, facilitate professional support from psychologists trained in workplace well-being.

Build civility and voice together. Some people mistake authenticity for a license to be cruel. Others try to buy “calm” by silencing people. Neither works. Understanding the four climates that emerge when civility and voice are combined can support the most functional organizational environment (Praslova, 2019):

Bullying of autistic people is a predictable outcome of systems that fail to check assumptions, reward sameness and conformity, and make targets prove their own reality. Workplaces that are healthy by design, with transparency, civility, and voice, on the other hand, not only support autistic people, but make work better for everyone (Praslova, 2024).
